Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hello, Doctor. Thank you so much for fitting me in today, I really appreciate it. I hope I'm not taking up time from someone who needs it more. 

Um... I suppose what brings me in is just that my sleep has become a bit... well, completely unmoored, I suppose. I don't want to make a fuss about it, as it might just be my age, you know? At first, it actually seemed quite positive. I was waking up after just two or three hours, but I felt perfectly fine. I was up drafting plans, feeling completely ready to go—very productive, really! 

But... well, lately, it's changed. Now I just lie awake long before dawn. My stomach gets all tight, and I just don't have any sense of rest at all anymore. 

Is... is that the sort of thing you need to know? I want to make sure I'm answering your questions correctly.

Well... I suppose it has a little, yes. I really hate to admit it, to be honest with you, because I don't want to sound like I'm complaining. I usually keep quite active in my retirement—volunteering at the museum, helping out with the arts council, keeping up with friends... but lately, well, I just haven't been showing up for them reliably at all. I feel simply dreadful about letting everyone down. 

And, um... well, my wife has actually had to take over managing our finances recently. I'm a bit embarrassed to say she’s even felt the need to stay home with me most of the time... just for safety, she says. I really don't want to be a burden to her, so I feel quite guilty about the whole thing. 

Does... does that make sense, Doctor? I hope I'm giving you the right kind of details.

A pattern? Oh... well, yes, I suppose there is, if I really look at it. I hope it’s alright if I explain how it’s shifted over time? I just want to be as thorough as possible for you. 

My sleep has become just... well, completely unmoored, I'm afraid. Anxious? Oh, um... well, no, I wouldn't use the word anxious.

Sad or down? Oh, well... no, I wouldn't use those words. Certainly not most of the time. 

I try very hard to keep my chin up, you know? Especially around the house. I wouldn't want to drag my wife down or... or be a gloomy presence for her. I just try to stay as positive as I can and keep moving forward. I hope that's alright to say? I mean, I wouldn't want you to think I'm just wallowing in self-pity or completely falling apart. I've always prided myself on being a fairly steady person... or at least, I try my very best to be. Does that make sense, Doctor? I hope I'm answering your questions properly.

I just want to make sure I'm giving you an accurate picture, and I don't want to mislead you if that's something I'm supposed to be feeling.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel tired most of the time?\n\nPatient: Tired? Oh, um... well, no, not really. I mean, I know I mentioned my sleep being a bit... erratic, but during the day I actually don't feel exhausted at all. \n\nI try very hard to keep my chin up and not just sit around, especially since my wife is doing so much for us right now. So, no, I wouldn't say I feel tired most of the time. Even with the early mornings, I still feel quite awake. \n\nI... I hope that's not the wrong answer? I just want to be completely honest with you, even if it seems a bit contradictory. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any disturbing dreams or nightmares lately?\n\nPatient: Disturbing dreams? Oh... no, no, nothing like that. \n\nI mean, my sleep is a bit unpredictable, as I said, but when I do manage to drift off, I don't have any frightening dreams or nightmares. I've always been quite fortunate in that regard, I suppose. I don't want you to think things are worse than they actually are.\n\nIs that... is that what you were looking for? I just want to make sure I'm giving you all the right information so you can do your job properly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any pattern to when you wake up or have trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: A pattern? Oh... well, yes, I suppose there is, if I really look at it. I hope it’s alright if I explain how it’s shifted over time? I just want to be as thorough as possible for you. \n\nMy sleep has become just... well, completely unmoored, I'm afraid. At first—and I really didn't think it was a problem at the time because I felt so wonderful—I was waking up after only two or three hours. I'd be right out of bed, drafting up all sorts of plans, just feeling completely ready to go. I actually thought I was just being highly productive, you know? So I certainly didn't want to make a fuss or complain about it then. \n\nBut lately... the pattern has changed. Now I just find myself lying awake long before dawn. And it's not like before at all. My stomach gets all tight, and I just... I have absolutely no sense of rest. I feel a bit embarrassed even admitting it, because I know I should be managing my schedule better for my wife's sake, but that's honestly how it's been. \n\nIs that... is that the sort of pattern you were asking about, Doctor? I hope I'm not rambling too much, I just want to make sure I'm giving you exactly what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you lie awake at night?\n\nPatient: Anxious? I'm mostly just lying there thinking about the day ahead, or, you know, hoping I don't disturb my wife while she's trying to sleep. I certainly don't want to give you the impression that I'm lying there in a panic or anything like that. I manage it quite well, really.\n\nI just... I try to stay calm and quiet. I hope that makes sense? I'd hate for you to think I'm falling to pieces over a bit of lost sleep. An investment loss. I mean, it was quite substantial, unfortunately. Pleasure in activities? Oh, um... well, I suppose that's a... a fair question. 

I try to keep busy, of course. I wouldn't want to just sit around the house all day being a bother. But... well, if I'm being completely honest—and I hope it's alright to admit this—things haven't been quite the same lately. 

I used to love spending whole afternoons at the museum, or, you know, I'd really lose myself in photography and cooking. It was wonderful. But recently... I don't know, I look at my camera, or even the bridge table, and I just... I don't feel much of anything. It's mostly just... well, it feels like a lot of effort just to pretend I'm enjoying it. 

I feel terrible saying that, really. I know I should be making more of an effort to enjoy my retirement and be good company. I just... I hope you don't think I'm being ungrateful for the free time I have. I'm sure it's just a phase I need to push through. Am I making sense, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of how things are.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, yes... I suppose it's very much like what I was just trying to explain. I hope I didn't confuse you a moment ago, I certainly didn't mean to make you ask twice! 

But... yes, "interest" is probably the perfect word for it. As I mentioned, I used to spend whole afternoons at the museum, or I'd completely lose myself in my photography and cooking. It used to bring me such joy. But lately, when I look at my camera sitting there, or... or when I sit down at the bridge table, I just feel absolutely nothing. Well, nothing except the sheer effort it would take to pretend I still care about it. 

I feel so terribly guilty saying that out loud. I know I should be more engaged, and I really am trying my absolute best to stay active and be a good sport about it all. Does that clarify things a bit better, Doctor? I want to make sure I'm giving you the exact information you need.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Worthless or guilty? Oh, my. Well... I suppose we all have regrets, don't we? It's only natural to look back and wish we'd done things a bit differently. 

But... well, since you ask, and I do want to be completely open with you... I have been struggling quite a bit with... with a certain decision I made. I mentioned the financial situation earlier, and... well, to be perfectly honest, it weighs on me terribly. I find myself just sitting there, replaying that whole investment decision over and over in my head. 

I try not to let my wife see it, of course. I wouldn't want her to worry any more than she has to. But... I just keep telling myself that I've completely ruined her security. After a lifetime of always being the responsible one, the one who took care of everything... I feel like I've just made an absolute fool of myself. 

I'm so sorry, I don't mean to sound so dramatic. I know I need to just pull myself together and move on. It's just hard to shake that feeling that I've let everyone down. I hope you don't think less of me for admitting that, Doctor. Is it... is it normal to get so stuck on one mistake like that?

I don't want to be a complainer or make mountains out of molehills. So, no, not excessive worry, I don't think. \n\nI hope that's an alright answer? Oh... weight loss? Well, actually, I'm quite relieved to say that hasn't been an issue. I hope that's a good sign?

Well, I... I suppose "depressed" is a rather strong word, isn't it? I wouldn't want you to think I'm just sitting around weeping all day or completely falling to pieces. I really am trying to keep a brave face on for my wife.

I really hope I haven't been dragging this out too long, I know I should have probably tried to snap out of it sooner. \n\nBut, um... if I'm thinking back, I'd say this... this low feeling, the pulling away from things and feeling so badly about the money... it's been going on for about nine months. Yes, right around nine months, ever since those plans fell apart. \n\nI really am trying my best to get past it, I promise. I don't want to be a burden or seem like I'm just wallowing in it. Do you think nine months is... well, is it terribly unusual to feel this way for that long? My wife still prepares our meals, and I always make absolutely sure to finish whatever she puts in front of me. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I'd like to say I've had some good stretches, I really would. I know it sounds so dreary to say it's been constant. I don't want to seem like I'm not trying to pull myself out of it. 

But, if I'm looking back over the whole nine months... it's been pretty steady. I mean, maybe a day here or there where it doesn't feel quite as heavy, but... no, I haven't really had any periods where I felt like my old self again. It's just been this... this same sort of low feeling, day in and day out. 

I'm so sorry, I know that must be frustrating to hear when you're trying to help me. I promise I'm not doing it on purpose. Does that make sense, Doctor? I hope I'm explaining it right.

I hope I'm not oversharing or making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Sad or down? Oh, well... no, I wouldn't use those words. Certainly not most of the time. \n\nI try very hard to keep my chin up, you know? Especially around the house. I wouldn't want to drag my wife down or... or be a gloomy presence for her. I suppose I do feel a bit foolish about the financial situation, like I mentioned, but I'm certainly not sitting around weeping or anything of that sort. \n\nI just try to stay as positive as I can and keep moving forward. I hope that's alright to say? I mean, I wouldn't want you to think I'm just wallowing in self-pity or completely falling apart. I've always prided myself on being a fairly steady person... or at least, I try my very best to be. Does that make sense, Doctor? Oh, um... well, I suppose that's a... a fair question. \n\nI try to keep busy, of course. I wouldn't want to just sit around the house all day being a bother. I hope I didn't confuse you a moment ago, I certainly didn't mean to make you ask twice! \n\nBut... yes, \"interest\" is probably the perfect word for it. Well, nothing except the sheer effort it would take to pretend I still care about it. \n\nI feel so terribly guilty saying that out loud. I know I should be more engaged, and I really am trying my absolute best to stay active and be a good sport about it all. Does that clarify things a bit better, Doctor? I want to make sure I'm giving you the exact information you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worthless or guilty about things that have happened?\n\nPatient: Worthless or guilty? Oh, my. Well... After a lifetime of always being the responsible one, the one who took care of everything... Yes, right around nine months, ever since those plans fell apart. \n\nI really am trying my best to get past it, I promise. I don't want to be a burden or seem like I'm just wallowing in it. I try to shake it off, I really do, but it's just... it's just always there, in the background. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that's probably not what you want to hear. I should be doing better at my age, shouldn't I? I hope you don't think I'm just feeling sorry for myself.
